review_text: The experience at Galway was pretty good.   Food was ok.  Nothing spectacular, and perhaps just a little above average.   The fish and chips were okay.  The fish did come out hot, and it was fresh tasting but it was also a tad bland.   I'm not a big fan of salt, but it definitely needed more.   The chips were a big disappointment.  They were cut so thin there wasn't much potato in there between the crispy outer parts.... The coleslaw was bad.   I've never had coleslaw that had such a strong taste of onion.  I only had one bite, as did my companion.  It was probably the worst coleslaw I've ever had.

Drinks were definitely on the high end in prices.   20 bucks for a chocolate type martini? That's too high.  Nine bucks for a draft of Guinness.  Overall, just an average experience.

Service was efficient and friendly.

rating: 4
review_text: Great find in Gulf shores, not the typical tourist trap. Not the cheapest place in the area a little on the pricier side, but a great value for the quality of food and service received. Service was excellent, not overbearing, inattentive, or rude. Frozen Irish coffees were perfect. The pretzel with beer cheese and curry mustard was probably the best we've ever had. The lamb chops were excellent, my wife, who usually doesn't care for lamb even liked them. The fish and chips was almost as good as what you would get in the UK, came out hot and crisp but didn't maintain the crispness of the real deal. Our only complaint is that everything on the menu looked so good and we would love to go back and try something else.

--- entity_reviews [row 2 of 5] ---
id: 63f404d6-42ff-41ac-8af0-1dd632aa4b88
entity_slug: the-galway-irish-public-house
reviewer_name: G Man
rating: 3
body: The experience at Galway was pretty good.   Food was ok.  Nothing spectacular, and perhaps just a little above average.   The fish and chips were okay.  The fish did come out hot, and it was fresh tasting but it was also a tad bland.   I'm not a big fan of salt, but it definitely needed more.   The chips were a big disappointment.  They were cut so thin there wasn't much potato in there between the crispy outer parts.... The coleslaw was bad.   I've never had coleslaw that had such a strong taste of onion.  I only had one bite, as did my companion.  It was probably the worst coleslaw I've ever had.

Drinks were definitely on the high end in prices.   20 bucks for a chocolate type martini? That's too high.  Nine bucks for a draft of Guinness.  Overall, just an average experience.

Service was efficient and friendly.

entity_slug: the-galway-irish-public-house
reviewer_name: J McGaha
rating: 4
body: Great find in Gulf shores, not the typical tourist trap. Not the cheapest place in the area a little on the pricier side, but a great value for the quality of food and service received. Service was excellent, not overbearing, inattentive, or rude. Frozen Irish coffees were perfect. The pretzel with beer cheese and curry mustard was probably the best we've ever had. The lamb chops were excellent, my wife, who usually doesn't care for lamb even liked them. The fish and chips was almost as good as what you would get in the UK, came out hot and crisp but didn't maintain the crispness of the real deal. Our only complaint is that everything on the menu looked so good and we would love to go back and try something else.
